Replay attacks: an adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} could reuse the previous messages captured from a public channel to participate in the current sessions. However, each step comprises the random fresh nonces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{n_i},{n_j},{n_{ij}}} \right\}$$\end{document} and timestamp \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{T_1},{T_2},{T_3},{T_4}} \right\}$$\end{document}. Assuming that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} has stored the messages from the last time, then it tries to utilize these messages in exchanging with other entities during the authentication and key agreement phase. In our scheme, every new message \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {msg_i^{new}} \right\}$$\end{document} is masked with a symmetric cryptosystem using Chebyshev chaotic map and timestamp \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${T_i}$$\end{document}, and it is verified upon receipt. Thus, the adversary cannot have any advantage in passing the verification, and the scheme is immune to these attacks.

Replay attacks: an adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} could reuse the previous messages captured from a public channel to participate in the current sessions. However, each step comprises the random fresh nonces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{n_i},{n_j},{n_{ij}}} \right\}$$\end{document} and timestamp \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{T_1},{T_2},{T_3},{T_4}} \right\}$$\end{document}. Assuming that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} has stored the messages from the last time, then it tries to utilize these messages in exchanging with other entities during the authentication and key agreement phase. In our scheme, every new message \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {msg_i^{new}} \right\}$$\end{document} is masked with a symmetric cryptosystem using Chebyshev chaotic map and timestamp \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${T_i}$$\end{document}, and it is verified upon receipt. Thus, the adversary cannot have any advantage in passing the verification, and the scheme is immune to these attacks.

ML-based modeling attack: in the ML modeling attack, an adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} accumulates numbers of exchanging data sets like \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ {{R_{is}},{C_{is}}} \right\} _{s \in {Z^ + }}}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ {ms{g_{ik}}} \right\} _{k \in {Z^ + }}}$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ {HI{D_{is}}} \right\} _{s \in {Z^ + }}}$$\end{document}, where k, s represent the number of times exchanging data is collected for the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${i^{th}}$$\end{document} entity, respectively. In our scheme, these exchanging data sets \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ {ms{g_{ik}}} \right\} _{k \in {Z^ + }}}$$\end{document} are masked by a symmetric key encryption such as advanced encryption standard (AES) algorithm, and pseudo identity set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\left\{ {HI{D_{is}}} \right\} _{s \in {Z^ + }}}$$\end{document} are concealed for the hash operator and PUF function. Therefore, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} cannot have any advantage in cryptanalysis utilizing ML modeling attack. Additionally, if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} uses the developed mathematical model based on the CRP set and successfully predicts the PUF response \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${R_{is}}$$\end{document} to a new challenge \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${C_{is}}$$\end{document}, also cannot infer the sensitive message without the credentials \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{M_i},{\tau _i}} \right\}$$\end{document}. Consequently, our scheme can resist this type of attack, because adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} cannot successfully obtain the sensitive messages stored in entities’ memories by utilizing the ML-based modeling attack.

Impersonating attacks: in this type of attack, an adversary attempts to masquerade as the legitimate entity user by using the transmitted messages via the public channel. In the authentication subsection, the adversary cannot obtain the random nonces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{n_j},{R_{j0}},{n_i},{R_{i1}}} \right\}$$\end{document} with the properties of PUF, XOR operator, hash function, and the symmetric cryptosystem. The temporary symmetric keys \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {K_{U \leftrightarrow G}^i, K_{U \leftrightarrow G}^{i + 1}, K_{S \leftrightarrow G}^j} \right\}$$\end{document} are protected by properties of the Chebyshev chaotic map, thereby, the adversary can’t compute these parameters. As a result, the scheme is secure against the impersonating attack since the correct messages of request and response cannot be generated within the authentication subsection.

Second, if an adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} registers in the wireless sensor network system as a legitimate sensor. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} also only obtains the information of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {HI{D_j},{Q_j},{C_{j0}}} \right\}$$\end{document} itself, because all the sensitive messages of the system are masked by the PUF. Therefore, our proposed scheme provides a strong defense against physical capture attacks.

Privileged insider attack: a privileged adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} can know the stored data \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {PI{D_i},I{D_i},{E_i},{R_{i0}},HI{D_j},I{D_j},{R_{j0}}} \right\}$$\end{document} from GW’s database and can record the secret data \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{M_i},{N_i},{Q_j}} \right\}$$\end{document} transmitted during the registration process. Therefore, our proposed scheme is secure against privileged insider attacks.

Ephemeral key leakage attack: if an adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} can obtain the ephemeral keys \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{R_{i0}},{R_{j0}},{n_i},{n_j}} \right\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${n_{ij}}$$\end{document}. Nevertheless, also cannot compute the session key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$SK = {H_1}\left( {{n_i}\left\| {{n_j}} \right. \left\| {{n_{ij}}} \right. \left\| {{\Gamma _{s{k_i}s{k_j}}}\left( \omega \right) } \right. } \right)$$\end{document}. This is because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\Gamma _{s{k_i}s{k_j}}}\left( \omega \right)$$\end{document} is hard to compute without \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s{k_i}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s{k_j}$$\end{document} based on the Enhanced chaotic map computational Diffie–Hellman problem. Therefore, our proposed scheme is resistant to ephemeral key leakage attacks.

Ephemeral key leakage attack: if an adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} can obtain the ephemeral keys \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{R_{i0}},{R_{j0}},{n_i},{n_j}} \right\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${n_{ij}}$$\end{document}. Nevertheless, also cannot compute the session key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$SK = {H_1}\left( {{n_i}\left\| {{n_j}} \right. \left\| {{n_{ij}}} \right. \left\| {{\Gamma _{s{k_i}s{k_j}}}\left( \omega \right) } \right. } \right)$$\end{document}. This is because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\Gamma _{s{k_i}s{k_j}}}\left( \omega \right)$$\end{document} is hard to compute without \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s{k_i}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s{k_j}$$\end{document} based on the Enhanced chaotic map computational Diffie–Hellman problem. Therefore, our proposed scheme is resistant to ephemeral key leakage attacks.

Formal security analysis using real-or-random model

The Real-Or-Random (ROR) model35 is utilized to analyze the semantic security of our authentication and session key agreement scheme P. There three distinct participants \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${P^t}$$\end{document}, which are user \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} authentication and key agreement phase of the proposed scheme. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${t_1},{t_2},{t_3}$$\end{document} represent the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} Theorem 1

The polynomial-time advantage of adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document}, which aims to undermine the semantic security of the proposed scheme P, is represented as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Adv_\mathcal{A}^{AKA - WSN}$$\end{document}. Then, the advantage of adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} within the proposed scheme breaks the security of the session key SK can be expressed as:

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Adv_\mathcal{A}^{AKA - WSN} \le \frac{{q_H^2}}{{\left| {Hash} \right| }} + \frac{{q_p^2}}{{\left| {PUF} \right| }} + 2max\left\{ {C \cdot q_s^s,\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_1}}}}},\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_2}}}}}} \right\}$$\end{document}

where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${q_H}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left| {Hash} \right|$$\end{document} represent the number of queries to Hash oracle and the range space of hash function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$H\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document}, respectively. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${q_p}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left| {PUF} \right|$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$PUF\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document}, and the number of queries to Send oracle, respectively. Additionally, C and s satisfy Zipf’s parameters distribution36, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${l_1}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${l_2}$$\end{document} signify the number of bits in the biometric credential \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$BI{O_i}$$\end{document} and PUF secret, respectively.

Proof

To demonstrate the security of the session key, we design a sequence of games which is labeled as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_i}\left( {i = 0,1,2,3,4} \right)$$\end{document}. In each game, the probability of the adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} successfully winning the games is denoted as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Adv_{\mathcal{A}, G{M_i}}^{AKA - WSN}$$\end{document}. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_0}$$\end{document}: In this game, the adversary \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} is a real attacker and has full access to all oracles on this proposed scheme. Then, we can get the following result from the game: 1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{l} Adv_\mathcal{A}^{AKA - WSN} = \left| {2 \cdot Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_0}}^{AKA - V2G} - 1} \right| . \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} launches an eavesdropping attack by executing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {ms{g_4},{T_4}} \right\}$$\end{document} transmitting among \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} intercepted. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} wants to deduce the session key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$SK= {H_1}\left( {{n_i}\left\| {{n_j}} \right. \left\| {{n_{ij}}} \right. \left\| {{\Gamma _{s{k_i}}}\left( {p{k_j}} \right) } \right. } \right) = {H_1}\left( {{n_i}\left\| {{n_j}} \right. \left\| {{n_{ij}}} \right. \left\| {{\Gamma _{s{k_j}}}\left( {p{k_i}} \right) } \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, it executes \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Reveal\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Test\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} queries. The results of the output between the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Reveal\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Test\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} queries all need to know the private keys \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {s{k_i},s{k_j},s{k_{GW}}} \right\}$$\end{document} and the random parameters \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{n_i},{n_j},{n_{ij}},{R_{i0}},{R_{i1}},{R_{j0}}} \right\}$$\end{document}. Therefore, this eavesdropping attack cannot enhance the advantage that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} wins the game. However, the random nonces are unknown to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} during the authentication and key agreement phase; \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} only opts for a hash collision to calculate the session key in this game. Therefore, we can obtain the following result by using the birthday paradox37: 3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{l} \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_2}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_1}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| \le \frac{{q_H^2}}{{2\left| {Hash} \right| }}. \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_3}$$\end{document}: This game is not merely a similar condition presented in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_2}$$\end{document} but also an extension of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_2}$$\end{document}, wherein the PUF query is involved. Based on the analysis of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_2}$$\end{document}, the following result can be derived: 4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{l} \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_3}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_2}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| \le \frac{{q_p^2}}{{2\left| {PUF} \right| }}. \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_4}$$\end{document}: This is the final game, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} concentrates all its advantages on the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CorruptMD\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} query to extract the important nonces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{M_i},{N_i}} \right\}$$\end{document} stored in the mobile device. In terms of the sensitive values are represented as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${M_i} = {X_i} \oplus {H_2}\left( {I{D_i}\left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. } \right) \oplus {\alpha _i}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${N_i} = {H_2}\left( {\left. {PI{D_{is}}} \right\| {X_i}\left\| {p{k_i}} \right. \left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. \left\| {{\alpha _i}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${X_i} = {H_2}\left( {I{D_i}\left\| {s{k_{GW}}\left\| {I{D_{GW}}} \right. } \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\alpha _i} = {H_1}\left( {P{W_i}\left\| {{\sigma _i}} \right. \left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$PI{D_{is}} = {H_2}\left( {PI{D_{is - 1}}\left\| {{X_i}} \right. \left\| {{n_{is - 1}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}. Among all the aforementioned formulas, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} can only obtain the published pseudo identity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$PI{D_{is}} = {H_2}\left( {PI{D_{is - 1}}\left\| {{X_i}} \right. \left\| {{n_{is - 1}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, while the rest consist of the random numbers, passwords, and biometric key. With the help of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Send\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} query for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} to reveal user’s short-term private key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s{k_i} = {H_1}\left( {P{W_i}\left\| {{\alpha _i}} \right. \left\| {{C_{i0}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document} is computationally infeasible without the user’s \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P{W_i}$$\end{document} and biometric key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\sigma _i}$$\end{document}. Owing to the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${l_1}$$\end{document} bits biometric credential and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${l_2}$$\end{document} bits PUF values are computationally infeasible task for the fuzzy receiver, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} only guesses which is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{{{2^{^{{l_1}}}}}}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{{{2^{^{{l_2}}}}}}$$\end{document}. In conclusion, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_3}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_4}$$\end{document} are indistinguishable by executing the password and biometric guessing attacks. Based on Zipf’s law, the following result is given: 5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{l} \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_4}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_3}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| \le \max \left\{ {C \cdot q_s^s,\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_1}}}}},\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_2}}}}}} \right\} . \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Once all the results of games \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_0} - G{M_4}$$\end{document} are taken into consideration, the only thing that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} can do to win the game is to predict bit b by utilizing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Test\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} query. Thus, we can get the following result:6\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{l} Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_4}}^{AKA - WSN} = \frac{1}{2}. \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}By Eqs. (1), (2), and (6), the following formula can be figured out:7\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{l} Adv_\mathcal{A}^{AKA - WSN} = \left| {2 \cdot Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_0}}^{AKA - WSN} - 1} \right| = \left| {2 \cdot Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_1}}^{AKA - WSN} - 1} \right| = \left| {2 \cdot Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_1}}^{AKA - WSN} - 2 \cdot Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_4}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}According to (3), (4), (5), and (7) and the triangular inequality, we can get the subsequent result:8\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{l} \frac{1}{2}Adv_\mathcal{A}^{AKA - WSN} = \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_1}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_4}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| \\ \quad \quad \quad \quad \quad \le \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_1}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_3}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| + \;\left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_3}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_4}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| \\ \quad \quad \quad \quad \le \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_1}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_2}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| + \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_2}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_3}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| + \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_3}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_4}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| \\ \quad \quad \quad \quad \le \frac{{q_H^2}}{{2\left| {Hash} \right| }} + \frac{{q_P^2}}{{2\left| {PUF} \right| }} + max\left\{ {C \cdot q_s^s,\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_1}}}}},\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_2}}}}}} \right\} \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Finally, both sides of Eq. (8) are multiplied by 2 to get the desired result.9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} Adv_\mathcal{A}^{AKA - WSN} \le \frac{{q_H^2}}{{\left| {Hash} \right| }} + \frac{{q_P^2}}{{\left| {PUF} \right| }} + 2 \cdot max\left\{ {C \cdot q_s^s,\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_1}}}}},\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_2}}}}}} \right\} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

In terms of the sensitive values are represented as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${M_i} = {X_i} \oplus {H_2}\left( {I{D_i}\left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. } \right) \oplus {\alpha _i}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${N_i} = {H_2}\left( {\left. {PI{D_{is}}} \right\| {X_i}\left\| {p{k_i}} \right. \left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. \left\| {{\alpha _i}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${X_i} = {H_2}\left( {I{D_i}\left\| {s{k_{GW}}\left\| {I{D_{GW}}} \right. } \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\alpha _i} = {H_1}\left( {P{W_i}\left\| {{\sigma _i}} \right. \left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$PI{D_{is}} = {H_2}\left( {PI{D_{is - 1}}\left\| {{X_i}} \right. \left\| {{n_{is - 1}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}. Among all the aforementioned formulas, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} can only obtain the published pseudo identity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$PI{D_{is}} = {H_2}\left( {PI{D_{is - 1}}\left\| {{X_i}} \right. \left\| {{n_{is - 1}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, while the rest consist of the random numbers, passwords, and biometric key. With the help of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Send\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} query for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} to reveal user’s short-term private key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s{k_i} = {H_1}\left( {P{W_i}\left\| {{\alpha _i}} \right. \left\| {{C_{i0}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document} is computationally infeasible without the user’s \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P{W_i}$$\end{document} and biometric key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\sigma _i}$$\end{document}. Owing to the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${l_1}$$\end{document} bits biometric credential and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${l_2}$$\end{document} bits PUF values are computationally infeasible task for the fuzzy receiver, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} only guesses which is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{{{2^{^{{l_1}}}}}}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{1}{{{2^{^{{l_2}}}}}}$$\end{document}. In conclusion, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_3}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_4}$$\end{document} are indistinguishable by executing the password and biometric guessing attacks. Based on Zipf’s law, the following result is given: 5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{array}{l} \left| {Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_4}}^{AKA - WSN} - Adv_{\mathcal{A},G{M_3}}^{AKA - WSN}} \right| \le \max \left\{ {C \cdot q_s^s,\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_1}}}}},\frac{{{q_s}}}{{{2^{{l_2}}}}}} \right\} . \end{array} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$G{M_4}$$\end{document}: This is the final game, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} concentrates all its advantages on the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$CorruptMD\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} query to extract the important nonces \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\left\{ {{M_i},{N_i}} \right\}$$\end{document} stored in the mobile device. In terms of the sensitive values are represented as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${M_i} = {X_i} \oplus {H_2}\left( {I{D_i}\left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. } \right) \oplus {\alpha _i}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${N_i} = {H_2}\left( {\left. {PI{D_{is}}} \right\| {X_i}\left\| {p{k_i}} \right. \left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. \left\| {{\alpha _i}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${X_i} = {H_2}\left( {I{D_i}\left\| {s{k_{GW}}\left\| {I{D_{GW}}} \right. } \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\alpha _i} = {H_1}\left( {P{W_i}\left\| {{\sigma _i}} \right. \left\| {{R_{is}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$PI{D_{is}} = {H_2}\left( {PI{D_{is - 1}}\left\| {{X_i}} \right. \left\| {{n_{is - 1}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}. Among all the aforementioned formulas, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} can only obtain the published pseudo identity \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$PI{D_{is}} = {H_2}\left( {PI{D_{is - 1}}\left\| {{X_i}} \right. \left\| {{n_{is - 1}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document}, while the rest consist of the random numbers, passwords, and biometric key. With the help of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Send\left( \cdot \right)$$\end{document} query for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{A}$$\end{document} to reveal user’s short-term private key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$s{k_i} = {H_1}\left( {P{W_i}\left\| {{\alpha _i}} \right. \left\| {{C_{i0}}} \right. } \right)$$\end{document} is computationally infeasible without the user’s \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$P{W_i}$$\end{document} and biometric key \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${\sigma _i}$$\end{document}. Security proof using BAN logic

This section demonstrates the correctness of the proposed scheme for mutual authentication and session key agreement by implementing Burrows-Abadi-Needham (BAN) logic34,38, a widely used formal verification technique. The notations and descriptions of BAN logic are shown in Table 1. Idealized forms Information transformation over a public channel is based on the following idealized forms: M1: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${U_i} \rightarrow GW:{\left\{ {{n_i},{R_{i1}},{T_1}} \right\} _{\left. {{R_{i0}}} \right| {X_i}}}$$\end{document}; M2: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$GW \rightarrow S{N_j}:{\left\{ {{n_i},{n_{ij}},{T_2}} \right\} _{\left. {{R_{j0}}} \right| {Q_j}}}$$\end{document}; M3: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S{N_j} \rightarrow GW:{\left\{ {{n_j},{R_{j1}},{T_3}} \right\} _{\left. {{R_{j0}}} \right| {Q_j}}}$$\end{document}; M4: \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$GW \rightarrow {U_i}:{\left\{ {{n_j},{n_{ij}},{T_4}} \right\} _{\left. {{R_{i1}}} \right| {X_i}}}$$\end{document}.

Performance analysis

In this part, a comprehensive analysis of the performance comparison is conducted to prove the advantage of our scheme over the existing relevant schemes concerning both communication overhead and computational burden during the AKE phase. In addition, the analysis of the security and functionality features showcases that our scheme not only meets the vital security properties but also effectively mitigates potential security risks.

Comparison of safety features with related work.

Table 3 compares the security features and functional capabilities of the proposed scheme with those of existing introduced schemes for V2G networks. Note indicates that the scheme meets the security feature, while ’does not consider’ indicates the lack of support for or consideration of the security feature. According to “Authentication and Key Agreement Phase”, our proposed scheme can resist against various common security attacks, involving “SP1: User anonymity and privacy protection”, “SP2: Mutually authentication and key agreement”, “SP3: Perfect forward secrecy”, “SP4: Session key disclosure attack”, “SP5: Mobile device capture attack”, “SP6: Replay attacks”, “SP7: Impersonating attacks”, “SP8: Man-in-the-middle attacks”, “SP9: Off-line password guessing attack”, “SP10: Untraceability”, “SP11:Denial of service attack”,“SP12:Thwart ML-based modeling attack”, “SP13:Ephemeral key leakage attack”. It is observed that our proposed scheme surpasses other recent research in terms of security requirements and can effectively resist potential attacks. Communication cost analysis

In this subsection, we focus on the communication cost comparison analysis in the key agreement phase between our proposed scheme and existing schemes. To intuitively display the communication cost comparison, this paper assumes that the bit lengths for “system timestamp, identity information, random values, PUF challenge, response, the ECC point, the Chebyshev polynomial, symmetric encryption/decryption data length, and hash results” are defined as 32 bits, 160 bits, 160 bits, 160 bits, 160 bits, 320 bits, 256 bits, 256 bits, and 256 bits, respectively.

The proposed authentication and key authentication scheme involves the four necessary transmitted messages \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Ms{g_2} = \left\{ {ms{g_2},{T_2}} \right\}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Ms{g_3} = \left\{ {HI{D_j},ms{g_3},{T_3}} \right\}$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Ms{g_4} = \left\{ {ms{g_4},{T_4}} \right\}$$\end{document} with the following requiring sizes: Table 4 and Fig. 2 present a more visually communicative cost comparison, demonstrating that the transmitted expenditures of our scheme are relatively lower than those of other existing protocols in a similar context. Therefore, we can infer that the scheme can achieve higher security and functional characteristics while effectively reducing communication overhead.Fig. 2Comparison of transmission overhead.

The notations, descriptions, and execution time of devices.

In this subsection, a comparison analysis of communication costs between our proposed scheme and other existing similar protocols in the key agreement phase is presented. For convenience to measure the execution time of cryptographic operations, based on experimental data from reference39, the simulation environment yields the computational elapsed time in Table 5. Furthermore, we overlook the computation time of the XOR operation, as it is significantly lower than that of other cost operations. Additionally, based on the findings in38,39 and the comparison of execution time of various encryption schemes and hash functions40, we assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${t_{mp}} \approx {t_{fe}}$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${t_{puf}} \approx 0.7{t_h}$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$${t_c} \approx {t_h}$$\end{document}. Consequently, we used a personal computer equipped with an Intel i7-1165G7 2.80 GHz CPU and 16 GB of memory using Ubuntu 24.04.2 LTS to execute 1000 iterations of each cryptographic operation and measure the average execution time. And also, we utilize a HUAWEI Nova 13 Pro mobile phone with TSMC 6-nm Kirin 8000 processor having 12G RAM using HarmonyOS 4.2.0 to simulate the platform for resource-constrained User and Sensor.

The comparison results of the relevant simulation execution times are presented in the accompanying Table 5 and Fig.3. Through the above analysis, we observe that schemes22 and26 do not incorporate PUF operations, which leads to low computation overheads; however, they are unable to resist the physical cloning attacks. In23 and24, these schemes utilize hash functions and ECC point multiplication operations, which exhibit relatively high computational complexity and result in significant computational overhead. In25, the proposed scheme utilizes the certificates generated during the registration phase to derive the symmetric key, thereby reducing computational costs and ensuring the security of transmitted data. While many encryption schemes offer low computational costs, our proposed scheme stands out by demonstrating superior security, particularly in its resistance to temporary key leakage attacks. This is in contrast to other schemes that may still be vulnerable to various security risks.Fig. 3Comparison of computation overhead.

Comparison of computation overhead.

Conclusions

This paper introduces a privacy-preserving scheme for Wireless Sensor Networks (WSNs), which combines a Chebyshev chaotic map cryptographic mechanism with physical unclonable functions (PUFs). While existing studies have introduced authentication and key agreement (AKA) schemes based on cryptographic techniques to enhance security, these approaches may still be susceptible to certain vulnerabilities as evidenced by the forward security analysis of AKA protocols, which highlights the need for improved security measures. To address these limitations and facilitate secure communication within WSNs, we propose a novel an approach utilizing one-time random pseudonyms and dynamically updated authentication credentials to safeguard user privacy. The proposed scheme meets the rigorous security demands of WSNs and exhibits resilience against a broad spectrum of cyber threats. Moreover, the framework ensures fundamental security attributes, encompassing user anonymity, untraceability, perfect forward secrecy, and session key confidentiality. Performance evaluations indicate that the proposed framework not The system not only provides enhanced security features but also significantly reduces computational and communication overhead, as demonstrated by methods in distributed deep learning systems and communication systems. Future research will focus on supporting scalable (changes in device density and request volume) and decentralized solutions, further reducing computing and communication costs, and enhancing the applicability of the solution in resource-constrained environments.
